# Fact ledger: neighborhoods across the Grand Strand (batch 2026-10-b)

For page 1, "Best neighborhoods in Myrtle Beach, by what you want nearby" (`/buyers/best-neighborhoods/`). The rows also feed pages 2 (gated), 3 (no HOA) and 5 (affordable). One fact, one value: pages 2, 3 and 5 should cite these rows, not re-derive them.

Researcher: batch 2026-10-b researcher for page 1. Every source below was opened on 2026-10-13. Every row is marked "(added 2026-10-13)". The verifier columns are empty. **The writer may use rows marked `verified` only.**

Machine-readable summary: `batches/2026-10-b/data/neighborhoods.json` (one object per neighborhood, with the row numbers).

## Verification summary (2026-10-13)

The verifier did none of the research. Re-run on 2026-10-13: the county parcel queries (own paged pulls, own type rules), the whole AcclaimWeb deed index for 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026 (30,339 deeds, identical to the researcher's set), 130 deed detail pages (9 or 10 per area, all match on Book/Page, doc type, amount and record date), the resale medians and quartiles (all reproduce), 212 Real Geeks listing pages and 82 Beach Pro Team cards (0 fee differences; 10-second delay honored; robots.txt read), all 50 OSRM routes in the ledger (identical to the meter) plus nearest-access, nearest-store and nearest-hospital tests, county flood counts for 14 areas (identical) plus about 75 FEMA NFHL point queries, and the school zone lookups at the reference point and at every home parcel. Never opened Zillow.

**Counts:** 164 rows checked. Verified 154. Wrong 10. Unverifiable 0. Rows 35 to 45 (The Farm) were left to the other verifier and are untouched. "Verified" here means the number reproduces; the note on most rows adds a limit the page must keep.

**Wrong:**

- Row 50: lists '[private owner, name removed]' (a private owner, matched on 'HOA' inside 'Hoang') as an owners' association. Remove.
- Row 62: lists '[private owner, name removed]' (a private owner, matched on 'POA' inside 'Poat'). Remove.
- Row 107: 'Southern Land & Golf Company Ltd' is a company, not an association.
- Row 145: Prince Creek has 193 parcels with a building value, not 258. 65 'townhouse' parcels have no building value.
- Row 173: the Tidewater amenity parcel has $329,604 of improvements on the roll; 'what is built is not in the record' misleads.
- Row 175: the 'Cherry Grove Beach' cards are lots (12) and no-bedroom listings (6) from 2021 to 2026; they say nothing about HOA fees for homes.
- Rows 51, 63, 108, 163 (beach): the named access is not the nearest. Berkshire Forest is MYRTLE BEACH 40, 14.8 min, 6.5 mi. Plantation Lakes is 17.3 min, 7.8 mi (not 20). Tidewater is NORTH MYRTLE BEACH 97, 7.7 min, 3.1 mi (not 9.6 min, 3.8 mi). Wild Wing is MYRTLE BEACH 40, 20.9 min, 10.6 mi. The researcher picked the shortest of the 40 nearest accesses by straight line; the fastest by road was outside that list. All 362 Horry County accesses were tested here.

**The writer must not use:**

- Rows 50, 51, 62, 63, 107, 108, 145, 163, 173 and 175 as written. Each note gives the correct wording.
- A community's name on a figure that covers only some of its sections. The group rows (1, 12, 46, 113, 145, and the price rows) cover only parcels whose county description carries the name. Say "in these sections".
- Any resale low end: $70,000 in Pine Lakes (5106/827, building worth $340,378) and Deerfield (5113/87, same-surname parties), $55,000 and $74,168 in Cherry Grove. Use the middle half.
- A single "typical price" where the sample is small: Grande Dunes 7 resales, Prince Creek 10, Pine Lakes 17. Also by type: Tidewater condos 3, Cherry Grove condos 8, Forestbrook townhouses 5, Deerfield townhouse 1, Barefoot house 1 and townhouses 3.
- "Mostly home builders", "new homes" or any view of a builder or HOA from the deed index. It gives grantor names, not what they are or whether the home was new.
- Any reference-point address (private homes), owner names, or the names in the Row 175 street list.
- "Gated", "secure", "safe" from the listing text "Security" in a fee-includes field (row 17).
- The home counts as "homes" without "about": the counts are parcels with a building value, and 86 (Market Common), 108 (Cherry Grove), 64 (Barefoot) and 307 (Surfside) are not coded residential.

**Issues the writer must know:**

1. Group. Pine Lakes: the county has 243 more parcels described 'PINE LAKE', 'PINE LAKE PH 1B' and 'PINELAKE TOWNES' and 117 'GOLF COURSE SECT' parcels. With them the area has 32 resales, middle $535,000, against 17 and $600,000. Grande Dunes: 44 resales, middle $1,537,500 with the other sections, against 7 and $1,632,000. Barefoot: 45 resales, middle $510,000 with the other sections, against 23 and $430,000 (19 of the 23 are condos). Berkshire Forest: 50 resales, middle $368,000, against 24 and $388,000. Prince Creek: 57 resales, middle $490,000, against 10 and $422,450.
2. Mixed types. One price blends houses and townhouses in Market Common ($540,000 and $398,400), Forestbrook ($395,000 and $255,000), Prince Creek ($558,000 and $354,000) and three types in Surfside ($705,000, $405,000, $252,500). Give them apart.
3. Fees are a listing field entered by the agent ("HOA Fees (Calculated Monthly)"), not a rule, and no HOA document was read. Market Common's fee is a townhome fee (14 of 15 listings; the one house shows $101). Barefoot's is a condo fee ($670 to $704 on 8 of 10). Prince Creek, Deerfield, Forestbrook and Pine Lakes rest on 8 to 13 listings. Cherry Grove and Surfside have no fee figure.
4. Beach minutes are a no-traffic router estimate to an access on a list dated 2013 to 2019. Several nearest accesses have no parking: Grande Dunes (MYRTLE BEACH 136), Waterbridge and Plantation Lakes (MYRTLE BEACH 116), Cherry Grove (NORTH MYRTLE BEACH 129, 0.1 mile; the next with on-street pay parking is 0.3 mile), Tidewater's old pick (125), and Deerfield's (SURFSIDE BEACH 31, golf-cart parking only). "1 minute" in Cherry Grove is a drive, not a walk.
5. Time. Schools: the district's lookup still reads the 2025-26 zones; the 2026-27 year has begun and I found no 2026-27 rezoning. All home parcels in each area fall in one zone. McLeod Carolina Forest hospital: scheduled "in the coming weeks" on 2026-09-01; I found no opening notice. Re-check before publishing. The 'emergency department open since 2019' is true but the cited page does not say it (use mcleodhealth.org/?p=178463). Flood: FEMA map effective 2021-12-16. Listing counts were taken on the morning of 2026-10-13; Grande Dunes had 5 more North Village listings by afternoon.
6. Completeness. The deed index gives one parcel number per deed. 97 deeds of $10,000 or more countywide have none; three name these areas (5086/137 Berkshire Forest $525,000; 5090/1311 Wild Wing $432,000; 5100/870 Grande Dunes Promenade $692,830) and are in no count. Counts are a floor.
7. Grocery rows mean the closest of Food Lion, Walmart, Publix, Kroger, Lowes Foods, ALDI, Piggly Wiggly and The Fresh Market from OpenStreetMap, not "the closest grocery store". Three stores have no street address (Market Common, Barefoot, and Kroger's '1 North Kings Highway' is not plausible): name no address.
8. Hospital rows are the nearest of four acute-care hospitals. Free-standing emergency rooms are not counted.

### OK to use on the page as

The Farm (rows 35 to 45) is with the other verifier. Attribution goes in brackets; the writer puts it in the page's voice and keeps it out of the body.

- **Pine Lakes (The Preserve and Pine Lakes Estates).** Price: About 17 resales sold between October 2025 and September 2026, mostly between $540,000 and $670,000 [Horry County deed index]. Fee: Listings show $95 to $98 a month for houses in The Preserve [MLS listing field, 12 homes, October 2026]; the older Pine Lakes streets list no fee [listings]. Beach: About 6 minutes by car to the nearest public beach access, 1.8 miles [router estimate, no traffic]. Flood: None of its 254 address points is in a FEMA high-risk zone [FEMA map effective December 2021].
- **Grande Dunes (named sections).** Price: A handful of resales (7) in the Villages, Villa Venezia and Bal Harbor sections sold between $1.19 million and $2.91 million; builders' new homes there sold from $530,000 to $1.07 million [deed index]. Fee: Listings show $199 to $400 a month for houses, most about $340 to $350 in North Village, and $325 to $386 for townhomes [MLS listing field, 45 homes]. Beach: About 9 to 10 minutes by car to a public beach access, 3.1 miles; the closest ones have little or no parking [router estimate; state access list]. Flood: 406 of 407 address points are outside FEMA high-risk zones; one is in zone AE.
- **Market Common.** Price: 46 resales sold at a middle of $503,750; houses at $540,000 (26) and townhomes at $398,400 (20) [deed index]. Fee: Townhomes list at $300 to $683 a month; the one house listed shows $101 [MLS listing field, 15 homes]. Beach: About 5 minutes by car, 1.9 miles. Flood: 827 of 838 address points are outside high-risk zones; 11, on streets such as Airforce Lane and Peterson Street, are in zone AE.
- **Berkshire Forest (sections the county names Berkshire Forest).** Price: 24 resales sold at a middle of $388,000, mostly between $347,000 and $440,000 [deed index]. Fee: Listings show $94 to $117 a month for houses, most $116 [MLS listing field, 18 houses]. Beach: About 15 minutes by car, 6.5 miles. Flood: None of 983 address points is in a high-risk zone.

## Deciding fact (reported first)

The plan needs 8 to 12 named neighborhoods, each with a typical sale price, an HOA fee (or none) and minutes to the beach. Result: **15 neighborhoods have a recorded typical resale price and beach minutes. 13 have an HOA fee from listings.** Two have no HOA fee figure: Cherry Grove (listing cards under one MLS name show none, row 175) and Surfside Beach (no listing under one MLS name). Four price samples are small (Grande Dunes 7 resales, Prince Creek 10, The Farm 16, the Pine Lakes group 17; see Not verified, item 8). The four 55+ pages are included by reference (section "55+ communities by reference").

## Method notes for the verifier

- **Which neighborhoods.** 15 named places across the main areas: Myrtle Beach (Pine Lakes, Grande Dunes, The Market Common), Carolina Forest (The Farm, Berkshire Forest, Plantation Lakes, Waterbridge), Forestbrook (Forestbrook Estates), North Myrtle Beach (Cherry Grove, Tidewater Plantation, Barefoot Resort), Surfside Beach (Deerfield Plantation area; the beach-side streets of the town), Murrells Inlet (Prince Creek) and Conway (Wild Wing Plantation). Chosen for search demand (names in the task and the plan) and for a spread of prices, HOA and no HOA, golf and new construction.
- **Group = the county's name, not the marketing name.** Each neighborhood is the set of county parcels whose legal description carries the name (HorryCountyGISApp layer 24), with the filter in its first row. Several are narrower or wider than the name people use. Read each first row before using the price:
  - *The Farm*: "THE FARM @ TIMBERLAKE" (near Surfside, 265 parcels) and "THE FARM AT FOX BAY" (near Loris) are different places and are left out. Page 4's researcher should use the same 273-parcel set.
  - *Pine Lakes*: the county name "PINE LAKES" covers The Preserve at Pine Lakes (phases 1A, 2, 3; it has an HOA) and Pine Lakes Estates. The older streets by the club are "GOLF COURSE SECT" and similar, and are not in the price.
  - *Grande Dunes*: only the sections whose description names Grande Dunes (The Villages, Bal Harbor, Village, Villa Venezia, Promenade, the parcels east of Grande Dunes Blvd). Most of Grande Dunes is filed under other names (Cipriana, Members Club, Seville, Siena Park, Palermo, Milano, Cadiz and others), and Del Webb is "MARINA TRACT". "GRANDE DUNES N"/"GRANDE DUNES NORTH" (tax district 550, near 33.79, -78.79) is a different place and is left out.
  - *Barefoot Resort*: only parcels named Barefoot Resort or Barefoot Village (Yacht Club Villas, North Tower, Dye Townhomes, The Retreat). Barefoot Resort's many other condo and house sections carry their own names and are not in the price. It is mostly condos.
  - *Surfside Beach*: parcels inside the town limits (OpenData Municipalities) on the ocean side of US 17 Business (county Highways layer 17), by a point-side test on the parcel centroid. It is most of the town.
  - *Prince Creek*: Seasons at Prince Creek (55+, its own page) is left out.
- **Prices.** Same method as batch 2026-10-a (`templates/research-brief.md`): AcclaimWeb Doc Type DEED (001), recording dates 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026 (30,339 deeds countywide, the same count as batch 2026-10-a; index "Released through date: 10/04/2026"), matched by ParcelNumber. Dropped: consideration under $10,000; corrective or re-recorded deeds; deeds that convey several lots or units (memo or legal says "SEVERAL", "LOTS" or "n & n"); deeds from a town, city or county; tract or remainder parcels; business or industrial units; deeds to a builder. Sales by any company that sold 4 or more in the group, or by a named builder (NVR, D.R. Horton, Lennar, Pulte, Dream Finders, Sands Building Group, RS Parker, Waterbridge SC LLC), are reported apart. Parcels with no building value on the roll are reported apart as "land". The middle half uses quartiles, inclusive method. Lowest and highest sales were opened on their detail pages; all other sales are listed by Book/Page.
- **Types.** condo = land use 107 or "HPR", "U/APT/CONDO" or "UNIT" in the legal description; townhouse = land use 109 or "TWNH"/"TOWNH"; land = MarketImprv 0; house = the rest. The county publishes no land use key online; the codes were read from examples.
- **Reference point.** For each neighborhood, the county address point (layer 22) on a parcel of the group nearest the mean centroid of its home parcels. It is a home address, used only as a start point; the page should say "from the middle of the neighborhood", not the address.
- **Distances.** OSRM public router (router.project-osrm.org), table call to choose, route call for the figure. Beach: S.C. Department of Environmental Services beach access layer, public accesses run by a city, town or county (state parks left out; the layer's records date from 2013 to 2019). Grocery: OpenStreetMap supermarkets of the named chains (Overpass, maps.mail.ru mirror; overpass-api.de reset the connection). Hospital: the four open acute-care hospitals, points reused from batch 2026-10-a (places.json, verified there). McLeod Health Carolina Forest's new hospital was not open by any source found; where it is closer, a separate row says so.
- **Flood.** The county Flood Zones layer 19 (FEMA zones) for every address point, then FEMA's own NFHL layer 28 at the reference point of all 15 and at 4 points inside mapped zones. hazards.fema.gov refused curl from this environment (TLS reset), so the FEMA checks were opened with WebFetch; each URL is a point query that opens in a browser. All 18 FEMA answers matched the county layer: reference points X (Surfside's X shaded, 0.2 percent), except Cherry Grove's, which is AE; the 4 in-zone checks (323 48th Ave N, 122 15th Ave N, 1330 Airforce Ln, and Cherry Grove's reference point) returned AE, SFHA_TF "T".
- **Schools.** Horry County Schools Attendance Zone Lookup layers, point query at the reference point. Names only.
- **HOA fees.** The brief's listing exception: Real Geeks (mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com) active listings by MLS neighborhood name, each listing page read for "HOA Fees (Calculated Monthly)", and The Beach Pro Team subdivision cards (crawl delay 10 seconds honored) for the same MLS numbers. robots.txt allows both paths for every agent. Both repeat one MLS field, so agreement checks transcription only. The MLS names used are in each row's URL.
- **Owner names.** County owner names appear only for organizations (associations, builders, golf companies). Builder and seller names in deed rows are companies.
- **Not recorded, by rule:** who lives anywhere, crime, "safe", "family", school ratings.
- **Work files** (scripts and raw pulls): the researcher's scratchpad `nbhd/` folder. The deed grid pulls are reproducible with the AcclaimWeb search named above.

The 273 reproduces (the row's filter returns 273 parcels, 262 with a building value) but it is only the parcels whose legal description starts 'THE FARM AT C F', 'THE FARM @ C.F' or 'BROOKBERRY @/AT THE FARM'. The county files The Farm under 14 legal-description wordings: also 'FARM AT CAROLINA FOREST' (461), 'FARM @ CAROLINA FOREST' (254 plus 304 with a PH number), 'FARM @ C.F. PDD PH 13' (73), 'FARM @ CF PH' (38), 'FARM @ C.F PH' (38), 'FARM @ C FOREST PH' (2), 'THE ORCHARDS PH 2' (117) and 'PLANTERS POINT' (74). Together 1,634 parcels, 1,571 homes (1,292 detached lots, 207 Orchards townhouse lots, 72 Planters Point condominium units); every one is inside one compact area (-78.886 to -78.867, 33.755 to 33.772) and has HOA-owned common land, tax districts 100 and 800. No Timberlake or Fox Bay parcel is in either set (Timberlake is at 33.63, -79.04, district 600; Fox Bay at 34.07, -78.87, district 220). Phases 1 to 26 appear in the covenant amendments (Phase 1, 18A, 18B, 19, 20, 21, 23B, 26 and the Parker land). The HOA's own FAQ says about 1,175 owners are 75 percent, which gives about 1,567 homes. 'Unincorporated Horry County' holds (no municipality in a box around all 1,571). Do not use 273 or 262. |

Note: their prices use the same deed window (October 2025 to September 2026) and the same method, so they compare with the rows above. Their beach minutes start at the community clubhouse or HOA office, not at a center address point.

## Waiting for MLS

- Median sale price and days on market by neighborhood from MLS closed sales, to replace or confirm the deed-index medians, mainly where the sample is small (Grande Dunes 7 resales, Prince Creek 10, The Farm 16, Pine Lakes group 17).
- Year built and home sizes by neighborhood (the county roll has no year built; listing pages do, but the brief allows listings for HOA fees only).
- The HOA fee for Cherry Grove and Surfside Beach streets, and the share of homes there with no HOA (page 3 needs it).
- Price per square foot.

## Search check

Not run as a separate step for this ledger. The plan (`batches/2026-10-b/PLAN.md`) lists the searches read on 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13. The rows answer the plan's must-answer list in this order: price (deed rows), HOA fee (listing rows), minutes to the beach, map point (reference rows), and the trade-offs a buyer meets (flood rows, builder activity, distance to a hospital). Not answered here: "walk or bike to the beach" as a measured walking time (only Cherry Grove and Surfside Beach are under 2 minutes by car; a walking route was not computed).

## Not verified

1. **Amenities (pool, clubhouse, trails) for most neighborhoods.** No HOA or builder page was found in a search for The Farm, Berkshire Forest, Plantation Lakes, Waterbridge, Wild Wing Plantation, Tidewater, Deerfield, Market Common, Forestbrook Estates or Prince Creek. Listing remarks describe pools and clubhouses (for example The Farm "resort style pools, a clubhouse, fitness center"; Waterbridge a lazy river and a clubhouse; Wild Wing a pool complex with a water slide; Plantation Lakes two pools, a clubhouse and a 170-acre lake; Berkshire Forest two amenity centers). Listings are allowed for HOA fees only, so none of this is a row. The "HOA Fee Includes" field on many listings names "Pool(s)" and "Recreation Facilities"; it is quoted inside the fee rows, not stated as a separate claim. What was tried: web search for each HOA name; grandedunes.com and barefootresort.com return 403 to curl and an empty page to WebFetch.
2. **Gated: not verified for any of the 15.** Listing remarks say Tidewater has "24/7 manned, gated security" and one Waterbridge listing says the community is gated, with gate hours. No HOA document, county record or builder page was found. Page 2's researcher should look for a primary source (HOA rules, county GIS gatehouse parcel, or recorded documents).
3. **55+:** none of the 15 was researched as a 55+ community, and no age rule was seen in the sources read. That is not proof that none has one; say nothing about age on the page for these 15.
4. **Golf at Grande Dunes, Wild Wing, Deerfield and Prince Creek.** No course page or county record was read. One listing calls a course near Deerfield "the former Deer Tract golf course". Leave golf out for these four unless a primary source is found.
5. **Builder and years built.** The deed index names the companies selling new homes now (builder rows). Who built the older sections and when was not found in a primary source.
6. **Store addresses.** Three of the chosen grocery stores have no street address in OpenStreetMap (Food Lion near The Market Common, Lowes Foods near The Farm, Food Lion near Barefoot Resort); the nearest county address points are 3890 S Kings Hwy, 215 Fresh Dr and 3924 S Hwy 17. Kroger for Grande Dunes is tagged "1 North Kings Highway" in OSM, which is not a plausible address; the county points nearby are 9600-block N Kings Hwy. Confirm on the chain store pages before naming an address.
7. **McLeod Health Carolina Forest hospital opening.** McLeod's 1 September 2026 release says "scheduled to open in the coming weeks". No source found that it has opened. Rows 9, 43, 54, 66, 79 give the drive only.
8. **Small samples.** Grande Dunes (7 resales, all over $1.19 million, while builders sold 54 new homes there with a middle of $678,500), Prince Creek (10), The Farm (16) and the Pine Lakes group (17). Say "few resales" or give the range.
9. **The Farm low sale.** The lowest The Farm sale, $140,000 (Book 5029, Page 1662), is between two people with the same last name and may not be an open-market sale. The method keeps every deed of $10,000 or more, as in batch 2026-10-a; the middle sale moves little without it. The same holds for $70,000 in Pine Lakes (5106/827), $70,000 in Deerfield (5113/87) and $55,000 in Cherry Grove (5053/1989).
10. **Surfside Beach HOA.** The beach-side streets of the town have no single MLS neighborhood name; no listing-based fee was gathered. County records show small associations inside the town (row 139).
11. **Cherry Grove flood.** 1,929 of 2,057 address points are in Zone AE and 84 in VE. That is the main trade-off there and needs no hedging, but flood insurance costs were not researched.

## Questions only the brokerage can answer

1. Which of these 15 do Chapter3 agents show most, and is any well-known name missing (for example Arcadian Shores, Prestwick, Indigo Creek, Avalon, Emmens Preserve or Withers Preserve, which were counted but not written up)?
2. For Grande Dunes and Barefoot Resort, which county section names do agents treat as part of the community? A list would let the price rows cover the whole community.
3. Are any of these gated with a staffed gate or a code gate today (Tidewater, Waterbridge, Grande Dunes sections, Barefoot sections)?
4. The HOA fee, and whether there is one at all, for Cherry Grove and Surfside Beach beach-side homes, from Chapter3 closing files or MLS.
5. Does Chapter3 want the McLeod Carolina Forest hospital named once it opens? It would be the nearest hospital for every Carolina Forest neighborhood here.

| 176 | (added 2026-10-13, round 2, tax) When a home in South Carolina is sold by deed, the county values it again at its fair market value for the next tax year, and the 15 percent cap on increases does not apply that year. So a buyer's tax is figured on about what the home is worth, close to the price paid. | reappraisal on an assessable transfer | S.C. Code 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b) and (B); 12-37-3150(A)(1) | https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c037.php | 12-37-3140(A)(1): "the fair market value of real property is its fair market value applicable for the later of: ... (b) December thirty-first of the year in which an assessable transfer of interest has occurred". (B): the fifteen percent limit does not apply "to the fair market value of real property when an assessable transfer of interest occurred in the year that the transfer value is first subject to tax". 12-37-3150(A): "an assessable transfer of interest in real property includes ... (1) a conveyance by deed". | 2026-10-13 | 2027-10 | verified with corrected wording | Re-opened https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c037.php on 2026-10-13 (2026-10-13). Same statute as affordable-facts.md row 107, which was verified today with corrected wording; its wording is reused. 12-37-3150(A)(1) lists 'a conveyance by deed' as an assessable transfer. 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b) sets fair market value 'applicable for the later of ... December thirty-first of the year in which an assessable transfer of interest has occurred'. 12-37-3140(B) says the 15 percent limit does not apply 'to the fair market value of real property when an assessable transfer of interest occurred in the year that the transfer value is first subject to tax'. The quotes in the row are exact. WRONG OR OVERSTATED in the row: (1) 'for the next tax year' is right only as the year the new value is first taxed; 12-37-3140(E) says changes in value from a transfer 'are first subject to property tax in the following tax year', so a 2026 buyer's new value first appears on the 2027 bill. (2) 'close to the price paid' and 'a buyer's tax is figured on about the price paid' are not in the statute. The statute sets fair market value (12-37-930: the price on the open market between a willing buyer and seller); it does not say the county uses the sale price. 'Close to the price paid' is an inference that holds for an ordinary open-market sale only (the-farm-facts.md row 85 allows it only as a labelled inference). Safe wording: When a home is bought, the county sets its value at fair market value for that year, and the 15 percent limit on increases does not apply to that new value; the new value is first taxed the next year [S.C. Code 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b), (B), (E); 12-37-3150(A)(1)]. In an ordinary sale that value is usually close to the price paid [inference, not stated in the statute]. Do not say 'your tax is based on the price you pay'. 2026-10-13. |

About 98 percent of Cherry Grove's address points are in a high-risk flood zone [Horry County copy of the FEMA flood map; FEMA NFHL]. A lender that is federally regulated, or whose loan is federally backed, must require flood insurance on a mortgage for a home in a high-risk flood zone [42 U.S.C. 4012a]. A cash buyer is not required to carry it. 2026-10-13. |

**The yearly cost lines on the page (writer's arithmetic, said as "about"; each input is a row):**

- Cherry Grove house: county tax for a home you live in $695,000 / 100,000 x $448.40 = $3,116 (rows 94, 176, 181; affordable row 65); flood insurance about $1,463 (row 179); HOA fee none on most homes (row 177). About $4,600 a year.
- The Farm house: county tax $352,000 / 100,000 x $367.60 = $1,294, plus the stormwater fee $89.40 = $1,383 (the-farm-facts.md rows 48, 77, 78; affordable row 72; row 176); HOA $107 x 12 = $1,284 (the-farm-facts.md row 5); flood insurance not required in zone X (the-farm-facts.md rows 71, 73), about $614 if bought (row 74). About $2,700 a year.
- Tidewater Plantation house: county tax $625,000 / 100,000 x $448.40 = $2,803 (gated-communities-facts.md row 43; affordable row 65, district 550 per row 102; row 176); HOA about $231 x 12 = $2,772 (gated row 20); flood insurance not required for most homes (row 111). About $5,600 a year.

| 182 | (added 2026-10-13, round 3, fee) Page: "About 400 properties in Cherry Grove also pay the city about $1,700 a year, on the tax bill, for dredging the canals. The city's bond for that work is scheduled to be paid off in April 2028." Basis: the City of North Myrtle Beach bills about 400 properties in its Cherry Grove Improvement District about $1,700 a year for the dredging of canals in Cherry Grove; the bond for the 2022 and 2023 dredging has a debt service schedule through April 1, 2028; this is the flat yearly fee row 181 found on Cherry Grove tax bills. | 2025-2026: $1,718.75 per Class 1 parcel (414 parcels), $1,185.94 per Class 2 parcel (2 parcels); bond through April 1, 2028 | City of North Myrtle Beach, City Council agenda of June 16, 2025, item 5B, "Resolution Approving CG Assessment 2025" (resolution and the Annual Special Assessment Report by Water Street Public Finance, the District's administrator) | https://www.nmb.us/AgendaCenter/ViewFile/Item/1251?fileID=3883 (agenda: https://www.nmb.us/AgendaCenter/ViewFile/Agenda/_06162025-453?html=true) | Resolution Section 2: "Land Use Class / Assessment Billed Per Parcel: Class 1 $1,718.75; Class 2 $1,185.94". Report: "The Annual Payment for 414 distinct Parcels classified as Land Use Class 1 for which the Special Assessment has not been prepaid equals $1,718.75." Recital: the 2022 Bond funds "the dredging of certain canals located therein in the years 2022 and 2023, the disposal of dredged materials, and matters incident thereto". Report: "The Series 2022 Bond has a debt service schedule through April 1, 2028. A prior City bond issuance in 2016, issued to fund an earlier dredging of the same canals, has been repaid in full." The District was created "By ordinance enacted on April 6, 2015, as amended by ordinances enacted on May 18, 2015 and on December 14, 2015". LIMITS: the resolution does not define Land Use Class 1 and 2 in the pages read (news reports call them single-family and condo); the 2026-2027 amount was not found; prepaid parcels are not billed. Row 181's $1,720.67 on 2026 bills is within a few dollars. | 2026-10-13 | 2027-07 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-13. Opened the city PDF myself (Item 1251, fileID 3883, agenda item 5B, resolution RES 25-09, adopted 2025-06-16). CONFIRMED: Section 2 bills Class 1 $1,718.75 and Class 2 $1,185.94 per parcel for the 2025-2026 Assessment Year; report: 414 Class 1 parcels and 2 Class 2 parcels billed (2 parcels prepaid and not billed; 415.38 equivalent units; total $713,934.16); the 2022 Bond ($3,242,000) funds dredging 'in the years 2022 and 2023'; 'The Series 2022 Bond has a debt service schedule through April 1, 2028'. CORRECTIONS. (1) 'On the tax bill' is NOT stated in the city document. It says only that the roll is filed with the Horry County Treasurer (Section 1) and, in the report, that the Annual Payment is 'the portion ... to be collected from such Parcel each Assessment Year'. Support for the tax bill is outside it: row 181 (county bills for tax year 2026 carry a flat 'Fees' line of $1,720.67 on 14 of 18 district-551 bills, matched to this fee by amount only, $1.92 off the city's $1,718.75, and the bill does not name it) and a 2015 pre-adoption notice that the assessment would be billed on county real property tax bills (secondary, reported by search; not read in full). I did not re-pull Treasurer bills. Keep 'on the tax bill' only as 'on the county tax bill' and only with row 181 verified; it is fair but inferred, so do not name the Treasurer as the source. (2) The FEE'S END IS NOT STATED. Only the bond's payment schedule ends in April 2028. The report says 'The Special Assessment has not been terminated'; the Annual Payment covers 'debt service on existing and potential additional bonds, and Administrative Expenses through the term of the District'; 'additional borrowings could be incurred by the City if funds were needed for the Public Improvements'; a 2016 bond for 'an earlier dredging of the same canals' was repaid and this 2022 bond followed. Appendix A-2 lists installments only through assessment year 2028 but says in a footnote they are 'the maximum capacity of the existing Special Assessment' and 'do not represent the existing debt service schedule, nor a projection'. So the page must not imply the fee stops in 2028. (3) The $1,718.75 is the 2025-2026 figure and is set each year (formula, lesser of the parcel installment or units x a rate capped at $2,400; the 2024 news reports give $1,821.07 for 2023-24 and $1,725 for 2024-25). The 2026-2027 city resolution was not found. A-2 shows the principal part rising (2026 $597,325; 2027 $732,474; 2028 $944,891), so later years may be higher; the document gives no projection, so say 'about $1,700 a year now', never 'stays'. (4) 'About 400 properties': 416 parcels are billed (414 + 2), 415.38 units. 'About 400' is fair; 'about 415' is closer. The 2 Class 2 parcels pay $1,185.94, so '$1,700' is the usual figure, not all. Class 1 and 2 are not defined in the pages read. SAFE WORDING: About 415 properties in Cherry Grove pay the city a yearly fee for dredging the canals, about $1,700 for 2025-2026, set each year [City of North Myrtle Beach resolution, June 2025]. It is billed on the county tax bill [row 181; inferred from bills]. The bond for the 2022 and 2023 dredging has payments scheduled through April 1, 2028 [city assessment report]. The city has not said the fee ends then; it can borrow again for the canals, as it did after 2016. |

for Cherry Grove and Surfside Beach, the no-HOA page's house set (no-hoa-facts row 69) matched by county PIN to the neighborhood's parcels. Per-listing data and method: batches/2026-10-b/data/best-neighborhoods-homes.json | https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/<MLS#>/ ; https://www.horrycounty.org/parcelapp/rest/services/HorryCountyGISApp/MapServer/24 | Fields as the listing agent typed them. These are homes for sale on 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14, not every home: new builder homes weigh heavily where builders are selling (Waterbridge, Grande Dunes, Wild Wing, Pine Lakes, Forestbrook Estates), so the page says 'houses for sale'. Small samples: Deerfield 6, Prince Creek 6, Barefoot condos 8, Forestbrook 8. Grande Dunes is the wider Grande Dunes search, not only the three gated sections, so the page says 'Across Grande Dunes'. | 2026-10-14 | 2027-01 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14. RECOMPUTED from data/best-neighborhoods-homes.json (own script, main kind only, land and other kinds dropped): every median reproduces. Year/size medians: tidewater 2002 / 2,688 (n 17); cherry-grove 1980 / 1,864 (38); barefoot-resort condos 2022 / 2,518 (8); wild-wing 2021 / 2,850 (25); waterbridge 2023 / 3,086 (27); grande-dunes 2026 / 2,860 (29); the-farm 2006 / 2,160 (15); plantation-lakes 2007 / 3,385 (15); berkshire-forest 2012 / 2,394 (18); pine-lakes 2022 / 2,419 (18); forestbrook-estates 2022.5 / 2,345 (8); market-common townhomes 2020 / 1,664 (14); deerfield 1983 / 2,046 (6); surfside-beach 2000 / 1,938 (27); prince-creek 2010 / 2,427 (6). Quartile years match the file (my percentile method is linear; Cherry Grove q1/q3 come out 1971/1988 against the file's 1970/1989, a rounding difference only). The file's 'n' counts drop land; the file's stats are otherwise identical to my recount. SPOT-CHECK: opened 34 property pages on mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com (robots.txt allows /property/; 10-second delay; no Zillow), 2 per neighborhood and 3 for Barefoot, Forestbrook, Deerfield and Prince Creek, chosen from the low, middle and high of each set by year built. All 34 match the file on Year Built, Total Heated SqFt and Property Type, and all show Status ACTIVE (so 'for sale' is true on the day read). Cherry Grove and Surfside use the file's 'mls' key; same result. The set for those two is every detached house (with or without an HOA fee) matched to county parcels, not the no-HOA subset. CHECK OF EACH LINE AGAINST THE DATA ('most' counted): tidewater 14 of 17 built 1999 to 2007, OK. cherry-grove 28 of 38 built 1960 to 1989, OK. wild-wing 19 of 25 built 2014 or later, OK. waterbridge 21 of 27 built 2023 or later, count OK but 'new' is wrong for homes up to three years old. grande-dunes 22 of 29 built 2024 or later; 24 of 29 show year built 2026 and one 2027, so many are not finished; a different set from the three gated sections. the-farm all 15 built 2005 to 2018, OK. plantation-lakes 10 of 15 built in the 2000s, OK. berkshire-forest only 10 of 18 built in the 2010s (5 in the 2000s, 3 in the 2020s), too tight. pine-lakes 15 of 18 built 2015 or later, OK. forestbrook 7 of 8 built 2021 or later, but n is 8. market-common 14 of 16 listings are townhomes, OK. deerfield 5 of 6 built in the 1970s and 1980s, n is 6. surfside 1960s to 2020s is the true range, OK. prince-creek built 2007 to 2015, n is 6. Every line says 'for sale'; none claims all homes. CORRECTIONS (page lines to change). (1) barefoot-resort: 'Most homes for sale here are condos, built in the 2000s or since 2020, with about 2,500 square feet.' The 8 condos are two groups, 3 built in 2002 at 1,801 to 1,825 sq ft and 5 built in 2022 or 2023 at 2,370 to 2,804 sq ft; a median of 2,518 describes none of the older ones and hides the spread. Replace with 'Of the 8 condos for sale, 3 were built in 2002 and have about 1,800 square feet, and 5 were built since 2022 and have 2,400 to 2,800 square feet.' (2) waterbridge: 'Most houses for sale here are new, built since 2023, with about 3,100 square feet.' becomes 'Most houses for sale here were built since 2023 and have about 3,100 square feet.' (3) grande-dunes: 'Across Grande Dunes, most houses for sale are new, built since 2024, with about 2,900 square feet.' becomes 'Across Grande Dunes, most houses for sale were built or are being built since 2024 and have about 2,900 square feet.' Keep 'Across Grande Dunes' and do not put it next to the price line for Calais, Palermo and Villa Venezia, which is a different set. (4) berkshire-forest: 'Most houses for sale here were built in the 2010s and have about 2,400 square feet.' becomes 'Most houses for sale here were built in the 2000s and 2010s and have about 2,400 square feet.' (15 of 18). (5) forestbrook-estates: 'Most houses for sale here are new, built since 2021, with about 2,300 square feet.' becomes 'Of the 8 houses for sale, 7 were built in 2021 or later and the middle one has about 2,300 square feet.' (sizes 1,903 to 2,656). (6) deerfield: 'Most houses for sale here were built in the 1970s and 1980s and have about 2,000 square feet.' becomes 'Of the 6 houses for sale, 5 were built in the 1970s and 1980s and the middle one has about 2,000 square feet.' (7) prince-creek: 'Most houses for sale here were built around 2010 and have about 2,400 square feet.' becomes 'The 6 houses for sale were built from 2007 to 2015, and the middle one has about 2,400 square feet.' (sizes 1,956 to 2,752). KEEP AS WRITTEN: tidewater, cherry-grove, wild-wing, the-farm, plantation-lakes, pine-lakes, market-common, surfside-beach lines. LIMITS for the writer: these are homes for sale on 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14 from one brokerage's search, not every home and not what the neighborhood is made of; builder homes weigh heavily in Waterbridge, Grande Dunes, Wild Wing, Pine Lakes and Forestbrook (say 'for sale', as the lines do); year built and heated size are as the listing agent typed them; a few 'built' years are 2026 or 2027, meaning not finished. Stale by 2027-01 is right; the listings change weekly, so rerun before any later republish. |
